{\mathcal{S}}[\phi]
has a rigid symmetry given by the action of
{\mathsf{D}}
on itself by left multiplication. Since the
R
-flux
R^{mnp}
vanishes in a split Manin pair
polarization,
p^{m}=\varrho^{m}
and
\tilde{\ell}_{m}
are the
left-invariant Maurer-Cartan 1-forms on
{\mathsf{G}}
, as discussed in
Section
6.1.1
. Since by Example
6.22
the metric torsion coefficients

f_{mn}{}^{p}
vanish in this case by the generalized isometry constraints, it
follows that

e=\mathds{1}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\varrho^{m}=\mathrm{d}x^{m}\ .

The Lie algebroid gauging of the Born
sigma-model in this case is achieved by the minimal coupling of the
Maurer-Cartan 1-forms
\tilde{\ell}_{m}
to a
{\mathsf{G}}
-invariant
connection 1-form
{\mathscr{C}}_{m}
, giving the gauged Born sigma-model actional functional

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}[\phi,{\mathscr{C}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\big(\delta_{mn}+\bar{b}_{mk}\,\delta^{kp}\,\bar{b}_{np}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{m}\wedge\star\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{n}+\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\bar{b}_{mn}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{m}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{n}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{4}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\big(\delta^{mn}+\bar{\beta}^{mk}\,\delta_{kp}\,\bar{\beta}^{np}\big)\,\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{m}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{m}\big)\wedge\star\,(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{n}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\big)

(6.24)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt-\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\bar{\beta}^{mn}\,\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{m}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{m}\big)\wedge\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{n}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,-\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\big(\bar{b}_{mk}\,\delta^{kn}-\delta_{mk}\,\bar{\beta}^{kn}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{m}\wedge\star\,\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{n}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\big(\delta_{m}{}^{n}+\bar{b}_{mk}\,\bar{\beta}^{kn}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{m}\wedge\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{n}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\big)\ .

Varying (
6.24
) with respect to the gauge fields
{\mathscr{C}}_{m}
leads to the self-duality constraints

\displaystyle\big(\delta^{mn}+\bar{\beta}^{mk}\,\delta_{kp}\,\bar{\beta}^{np}\big)\,\star\,\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{n}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\big)-2\,\bar{\beta}^{mn}\,\big(\bar{\tilde{q}}_{n}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt=\big(\bar{b}_{nk}\,\delta^{km}-\delta_{nk}\,\bar{\beta}^{km}\big)\,\star\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{n}-\big(\delta_{n}{}^{m}+\bar{b}_{nk}\,\bar{\beta}^{km}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{n}\ ,

which are formally solved by the non-local expression

\displaystyle\bar{\tilde{q}}_{m}+\bar{\mathscr{C}}_{m}=\bigg(\frac{1}{(\mathds{1}-\bar{\beta}\,\star)^{2}}\bigg)_{mn}\,\Big((\bar{b}-\bar{\beta})^{n}{}_{k}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{k}-(\mathds{1}+\bar{\beta}\,\bar{b})^{n}{}_{k}\,\star\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{k}\Big)\ .

Substituting this into the gauged action functional
(
6.24
) eliminates the dependence on
\bar{\tilde{q}}_{m}
, giving a reduced sigma-model that depends only on the leaf
space coordinates
x^{m}
. The complicated non-local dependence on the
bivector
\beta
owes to the appearence of
Q
-flux in the doubled
group background; nonetheless, the resulting physical background is
geometric.

We conclude this section by briefly considering some explicit examples which illustrate how this reproduces well-known backgrounds in the doubled formalism.

Example 6.25

(The Doubled Torus)
.

The simplest case corresponds to setting the structure constants to zero:

Q_{k}{}^{ij}=0\ ,

in addition to
f_{ij}{}^{k}=0
in (
6.20
),
so that
{\mathsf{D}}
is a doubled group integrating a Manin pair corresponding to the abelian Lie group

{\mathsf{G}}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\ .

In this case one finds
[
24
]

\varrho_{m}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,H_{mnp}\,x^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x^{n}\ ,\quad\tilde{\ell}_{m}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{m}\ ,\quad\beta^{mn}=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad b_{mn}=H_{mnp}\,x^{p}\ .

The reduction of the Born sigma-model then yields the standard non-linear sigma-model
S(g,b)
with flat metric

g=\delta_{mn}\,\mathrm{d}x^{m}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{n}

and Kalb-Ramond field
b
, so that the spacetime is locally
{\mathcal{Q}}=\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The
H
-flux of the
B
-field agrees with the field strength (
6.4
) of the fundamental 2-form in this case:

{\mathcal{K}}^{\textrm{\tiny L}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,H_{mnp}\,\mathrm{d}x^{m}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{n}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{p}\ .

After taking the quotient by a cocompact discrete subgroup
{\mathsf{D}}(\mathbb{Z})
, the spacetime becomes a
d
-dimensional torus
\mathbb{T}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{d}/\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
H
-flux in this split Manin pair polarization. Thus in this case the compact space
M={\mathsf{D}}/{\mathsf{D}}(\mathbb{Z})
reproduces the standard doubled torus in the geometric
H
-flux polarization
[
20
]
.

Example 6.26

(Doubled WZW Models)
.

Setting

H_{mnk}=c_{mi}\,c_{nj}\,Q_{k}{}^{ij}

in addition to
f_{ij}{}^{k}=0
in (
6.20
) recovers the doubled
sigma-model description of the Wess-Zumino-Witten (WZW)
model discussed in
[
103
,
104
,
25
,
67
]
. The doubled group is

{\mathsf{D}}={\mathsf{G}}\times{\mathsf{G}}\ ,

and if
{\mathsf{G}}
is compact then
{\mathsf{D}}
is embedded into the maximal compact subgroup
{\mathsf{O}}(d)\times{\mathsf{O}}(d)
of the generalized T-duality group
{\mathsf{O}}(d,d)
, where the
two copies of the semi-simple Lie group
{\mathsf{G}}
are associated
to the left-moving and right-moving worldsheet sectors. In this case we can set
\beta=0
using an
{\mathsf{O}}(d)\times{\mathsf{O}}(d)
-transformation, and embedding

{\mathsf{G}}
as the diagonal subgroup of
{\mathsf{D}}
, the gauged Born sigma-model
is a gauged WZW model based on the group
{\mathsf{D}}
with gauge
group
{\mathsf{G}}
. The field strength (
6.4
) yields the standard
H
-flux

H=-c_{mi}\,c_{nj}\,Q_{k}{}^{ij}\,\theta^{m}\wedge\theta^{n}\wedge\theta^{k}

for the reduced WZW model at level
1
based on

{\mathsf{D}}/{\mathsf{G}}\simeq{\mathsf{G}}
, where
\theta
is the left-invariant
Maurer-Cartan 1-form on
{\mathsf{G}}
. The Riemannian submersion from the
Born manifold
{\mathsf{D}}
to the quotient
{\mathsf{D}}/{\mathsf{G}}
is given by

{\mathit{\Pi}}:{\mathsf{D}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{G}}\ ,\quad(g,g^{\prime})\longmapsto g^{-1}\,g^{\prime}\ ,

where
g^{-1}
and
g^{\prime}
become the left-moving and right-moving
closed string fields after imposing the self-duality constraint
resulting from the gauged Born sigma-model.
Other natural choices of left-invariant almost para-complex structures on the doubled group

{\mathsf{D}}={\mathsf{G}}\times{\mathsf{G}}
correspond to subgroups of
{\mathsf{D}}
in the same conjugacy
class as the diagonal subgroup

{\mathsf{G}}\subset{\mathsf{D}}

[
25
]
. Discrete quotients of this
doubled group in the case
{\mathsf{G}}={\sf SU}(2)
are studied
in
[
25
,
66
]
in the context of the T-duals
of the 3-sphere
\mathbb{S}^{3}
, viewed as a circle bundle over the
2-sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

Example 6.27

(Drinfel’d Doubles)
.

The case of a Drinfel’d double

{\mathsf{D}}
which is a matrix Lie group corresponds to setting

H_{mnk}=0

in (
6.20
). The gaugeable Born
sigma-models in a Manin triple polarization, for which
f_{ij}{}^{k}=0
, single out the cotangent bundles

{\mathsf{D}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{G}}={\mathsf{G}}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{d}

of semi-simple Lie groups
{\mathsf{G}}
, which
reduce to a sigma-model description of flat space
T^{*}{\mathsf{G}}/{\mathsf{G}}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{d}
. In this case one has

\varrho_{m}=0\ ,\quad b_{mn}=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\beta^{mn}=Q_{p}{}^{mn}\,x^{p}\ ,

and the reduced sigma-model action
S^{\prime}(g^{\prime},b^{\prime})
can be expressed in terms of a
metric
g^{\prime}
and Kalb-Ramond field
b^{\prime}
defined through

g^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=(\mathds{1}+\beta)^{-1}
, or equivalently

g^{\prime}=(\mathds{1}-\beta^{2})^{-1}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad b^{\prime}=-(\mathds{1}+\beta)^{-1}\,\beta\,(\mathds{1}-\beta)^{-1}\ .

Alternatively, considering the change of polarization which
interchanges the roles of the Lie bialgebras
(\mathfrak{g},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathfrak{g})
in the Manin triple, which is the simplest example of a
Poisson-Lie T-duality
transformation, one obtains a sigma-model with

b_{nm}=\beta^{nm}=0

[
24
]
coinciding locally with that of
Example
6.25
with vanishing Kalb-Ramond field, as
expected from the general considerations of Section
5.2
.

7.
Born Sigma-Models for Doubled Nilmanifolds

A broad class of compactifications of string theory come in the form
of ‘twisted tori’, which are torus bundles that arise as
Scherk-Schwarz reductions with twist in the group of large
diffeomorphisms of the torus fibers. Examples include nilmanifolds,
which are quotients of nilpotent Lie groups by a cocompact discrete
subgroup. A more general class of examples consists of the
solvmanifolds that are discrete quotients of almost abelian solvable
Lie groups, which can be realized as torus fibrations over a
circle. Discrete quotients of the cotangent bundles of the underlying
Lie groups, which are Drinfel’d doubles, yield doubled geometries that
contain the original twisted torus as well as the correspondence space
for its geometric T-dual backgrounds, and are commonly refered to as
‘doubled twisted tori’. In this section we will consider the simplest and best
studied example which doubles the compactification on the
three-dimensional Heisenberg nilmanifold.

7.1.
The Doubled Twisted Torus

We shall first recall the
construction of the doubled twisted torus as a quotient space in the
case of interest here, following
[
24
,
39
]
; see e.g.
[
60
]
for more general cases. The doubled twisted torus is obtained from the quotient of the Drinfel’d double
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
of the three-dimensional Heisenberg group
{\mathsf{H}}
with respect to a discrete cocompact subgroup
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}).
The nilpotent Lie algebra of
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}={\mathsf{H}}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3}
has non-vanishing brackets

(7.1)

[T_{x},T_{z}]=m\,T_{y}\ ,\quad[T_{x},\tilde{T}^{y}]=m\,\tilde{T}^{z}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad[T_{z},\tilde{T}^{y}]=-m\,\tilde{T}^{x}\ ,

where
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Here the Heisenberg algebra
\mathfrak{h}
and the abelian Lie algebra
\mathbb{R}^{3}
are spanned, respectively, by
\{T_{m}\}=\{T_{x},T_{y},T_{z}\}
and
\{\tilde{T}^{m}\}=\{\tilde{T}^{x},\tilde{T}^{y},\tilde{T}^{z}\},
and together with
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}=\mathfrak{h}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3}
they form a Manin triple. Despite the fact that
{\mathsf{H}}
is not semi-simple, we can still give a matrix representation for the Lie algebra of the Drinfel’d double
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
. This will prove useful later on for explicitly writing down the coordinate identifications defining the global structure of the doubled twisted torus.

In local coordinates, any element
\gamma\in T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
may be written as

\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}1&m\,x&y&0&0&\tilde{z}\\
0&1&z&0&0&-\tilde{y}\\
0&0&1&0&0&0\\
0&-m\,\tilde{y}&\tilde{x}-m\,z\,\tilde{y}&1&m\,x&y+\frac{1}{2}\,m\,\tilde{y}^{2}\\
0&0&0&0&1&z\\
0&0&0&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}

where
(x,y,z)
are coordinates on the Heisenberg group
{\mathsf{H}}
and
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
are coordinates on the fiber
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then the left-invariant 1-forms are given by the Lie algebra components of the corresponding Maurer-Cartan 1-form

\Theta=\gamma^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma=\Theta^{n}\,T_{n}+\tilde{\Theta}_{n}\,\tilde{T}^{n}

as

\displaystyle\Theta^{x}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}x\ ,\quad\Theta^{y}=\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\Theta^{z}=\mathrm{d}z\ ,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{x}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}-m\,z\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ ,\quad\tilde{\Theta}_{y}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{\Theta}_{z}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}+m\,x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ ,

with dual left-invariant vector fields

(7.3)

\displaystyle Z_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\ ,\quad Z_{y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad Z_{z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial z}+m\,x\,\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\ ,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}}\ ,\quad\tilde{Z}^{y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}+m\,z\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}}-m\,x\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{Z}^{z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}}\ .

It follows from (
7.3
) that
\{Z_{n}\}
spans an involutive distribution
L_{+},
thus it defines a foliation whose leaves are given by the Heisenberg group
{\mathsf{H}}.
Similarly (
7.4
) tells us that
\{\tilde{Z}^{n}\}
spans an involutive distribution
L_{-}
whose foliation has leaves given by
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the fiber of the cotangent bundle

\pi:T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{H}}\ .

Since
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
is a Drinfel’d double, it is naturally endowed with a left-invariant para-Hermitian structure defined by the para-complex structure

\underline{K^{\textrm{\tiny L}}}=Z_{n}\otimes\Theta^{n}-\tilde{Z}^{n}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{n}

for which
L_{+}
is its
+1
-eigenbundle and
L_{-}
is its
-1
-eigenbundle. The split signature metric is given by

\eta^{\textrm{\tiny L}}=\Theta^{n}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{n}+\tilde{\Theta}_{n}\otimes\Theta^{n}\ ,

and the fundamental 2-form is

(7.5)

\omega^{\textrm{\tiny L}}=\Theta^{n}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{n}

with field strength

{\mathcal{K}}^{\textrm{\tiny L}}=\mathrm{d}\omega^{\textrm{\tiny L}}=-m\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ .

Comparing with (
6.4
) shows that the only non-vanishing flux
in this polarization is the metric flux
f_{xz}{}^{y}=-f_{zx}{}^{y}=-m
.

There further exists a unique left-invariant Riemannian metric
{\mathcal{H}}^{\textrm{\tiny L}}
on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
induced by the horizontal lift
g_{+}=\pi^{*}g
of the left-invariant Riemannian metric
g
on the Heisenberg group
{\mathsf{H}}
given by

(7.6)

g=\delta_{ij}\,\Theta^{i}\otimes\Theta^{j}\ ,

which can be written as

g=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&-m\,x\\
0&-m\,x&1+(m\,x)^{2}\end{pmatrix}

in the holonomic coframe
\{\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z\}
.
20
20

20

Here we slightly abuse notation as before and identify the coordinates on the Heisenberg group with the pulled back coordinates to
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}.
The left-invariant 1-forms on
{\mathsf{H}}
are identified with the left-invariant 1-forms
\Theta^{i}
in (
7.2
).

Then the basis
\{Z_{n},\tilde{Z}^{n}\}
of left-invariant vector fields on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
is orthonormal with respect to
{\mathcal{H}}^{\textrm{\tiny L}}
. The fiberwise metric on
L_{-}
is given by

g_{-}(X_{-},Y_{-})=g_{+}^{-1}\big(\eta^{\textrm{\tiny L}}{}^{\flat}(X_{-}),\eta^{\textrm{\tiny L}}{}^{\flat}(Y_{-})\big)

for all
X_{-},Y_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{-})
. Then the compatible generalized metric induced by the horizontal lift of
g
can be written as

(7.7)

{\mathcal{H}}^{\textrm{\tiny L}}=\delta_{mn}\,\Theta^{m}\otimes\Theta^{n}+\delta^{mn}\,\tilde{\Theta}_{m}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{n}\ ,

and it is easy to show that, together with
(K^{\textrm{\tiny L}},\eta^{\textrm{\tiny L}},\omega^{\textrm{\tiny L}}),
it defines a left-invariant Born geometry on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}.

The coordinate identifications defining the global structure of the doubled twisted
torus are obtained via the left action of a discrete cocompact subgroup
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})
of
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}.
Hence the left-invariant para-Hermitian structure of
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
remains well-defined on the doubled twisted torus

M_{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}\,\big/\penalty\,{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})\ .

A generic element
\xi\in{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})
is given by

\xi=\begin{pmatrix}1&m\,\alpha&\beta&0&0&\tilde{\delta}\\
0&1&\delta&0&0&-\tilde{\beta}\\
0&0&1&0&0&0\\
0&-m\,\tilde{\beta}&\tilde{\alpha}-m\,\delta\,\tilde{\beta}&1&m\,\alpha&\beta+\frac{1}{2}\,m\,\tilde{\beta}^{2}\\
0&0&0&0&1&\delta\\
0&0&0&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\ ,

where
\alpha,\beta,\delta,\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\delta}\in\mathbb{Z}
. The group
action on coordinates induced by the equivalence relation
\gamma\sim\xi\,\gamma
, which defines the quotient
M_{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}/{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})
, leads to the simultaneous identifications

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\sim x+\alpha\ ,\quad y\sim y+m\,\alpha\,z+\beta\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad z\sim z+\delta\ ,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\sim\tilde{x}+m\,\delta\,\tilde{y}+\tilde{\alpha}\ ,\quad\tilde{y}\sim\tilde{y}+\tilde{\beta}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{z}\sim\tilde{z}-m\,\alpha\,\tilde{y}+\tilde{\delta}\ .

This identifies
M_{\mathsf{H}}
as a
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
-bundle over

\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
, with base coordinates
(x,\tilde{x})
. The left-invariant 1-forms (
7.2
), together with the left-invariant vector fields (
7.3
) and (
7.4
), are invariant under the
identifications (
7.8
), hence they globally descend to the quotient
M_{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}/{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}).
This also means that the left-invariant para-Hermitian structure on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
descends to a para-Hermitian structure on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
, which we denote by
(K,\eta)
. Hence the corresponding eigenbundles
L_{+}^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
L_{-}^{\mathbb{Z}}
of
K

are both integrable, since their local generators satisfy the Lie
bracket relations (
7.1
); their integral foliations are
characterized, respectively, by the Heisenberg nilmanifold

\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}={\mathsf{H}}/{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})
and the
3
-torus
\mathbb{T}^{3}=\mathbb{R}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}^{3}

as leaves. This is an example of a transversely parallelizable
foliation
[
93
]
, which implies that the leaf holonomy is
trivial for all leaves, and hence
M_{\mathsf{H}}
admits the structure of a
Riemannian foliation.

Thus the Drinfel’d double structure here, in the polarization given by a Manin triple, induces a para-Hermitian structure
(K,\eta)
on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
. Furthermore, the left-invariant Riemannian metric on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
descends to a Riemannian metric on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
, that we denote by
{\mathcal{H}}
, which is still compatible with the para-Hermitian structure induced by the Drinfel’d double. We call
(K,\eta,{\mathcal{H}})
the
induced
Born geometry on the doubled twisted torus
M_{\mathsf{H}}.
In the following we demonstrate how to recover the well-known conventional sigma-model descriptions, in the framework of the Lie algebroid gaugings of Born sigma-models, from the various polarizations of the doubled twisted torus, thus reproducing the results of
[
24
]
from a different geometric perspective.

7.2.
Nilmanifold Polarization

We will first describe the Born structure defining the polarization whose leaf space is the Heisenberg nilmanifold
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
. For this, we note that the doubled twisted torus admits the structure of a principal torus bundle, which is inherited from the vector bundle structure of the cotangent bundle
\pi:T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}\to{\mathsf{H}}
: the typical fiber is
\mathbb{T}^{3}
acting freely on
M_{\mathsf{H}},
giving as base space
M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}^{3}\simeq\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
, i.e. there is a principal
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle
[
24
]

\bar{\pi}:M_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}\ .

The almost para-Hermitian structure induced by this
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-action is given by an isotropic splitting

\bar{s}:\bar{\pi}^{*}(T\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}})\longrightarrow TM_{\mathsf{H}}

with respect to the split signature metric
\eta
and the fundamental
2-form
\omega
of the short exact sequence of vector bundles

0\longrightarrow L_{\tt v}(M_{\mathsf{H}})\longrightarrow TM_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow\bar{\pi}^{*}(T\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}})\longrightarrow 0\ .

A compatible generalized metric
{\mathcal{H}}=g_{+}+g_{-}
is induced by the horizontal lift of the natural Riemannian metric
g
on the Heisenberg nilmanifold
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
which descends from (
7.6
).
Having introduced the Born structure on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
associated with this
nilmanifold polarization, there is a straightforward definition of a
corresponding Born sigma-model, as discussed in Section
4
, by considering a harmonic map
\phi:\Sigma\rightarrow M_{\mathsf{H}}
from a closed string worldsheet
\Sigma
to the doubled twisted torus.
This sigma-model is characterized by the pair
({\mathcal{H}},\omega)
with
{\mathcal{H}}
given by (
7.7
) and
\omega
given by (
7.5
).

The principal bundle structure of
M_{{\mathsf{H}}}
is crucial for describing the generalized isometry for the gauging of the Born sigma-model
{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{H}},\omega)
. The principal bundle

\bar{\pi}:M_{{\mathsf{H}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
with fiber
\mathbb{T}^{3}
admits a bundle-like metric given by (
7.7
).
Therefore we choose the vertical distribution
L_{-}^{\mathbb{Z}}=L_{\tt v}(M_{\mathsf{H}})
to be the Lie algebroid on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
of generalized isometries of our sigma-model. With this choice, it is easy to see that the generalized isometry conditions for a Born sigma-model are satisfied:

\pounds_{{X_{-}}}\,g_{+}=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\pounds_{{X_{-}}}\,\omega=0\ ,

for all
{X_{-}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{-}^{\mathbb{Z}}).

The Lie algebroid gauging discussed in Section
4.3
can then be applied to this case.
The Born sigma-model has a rigid symmetry under the left action of the doubled group
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}
on the coset
M_{\mathsf{H}}
. For this polarization we write it in the usual way as

{\mathcal{S}}[\phi]=\frac{1}{4}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\Big(\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\Theta}^{i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\Theta}^{j}+\delta^{ij}\,\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}_{i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}_{j}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\bar{\Theta}^{i}\wedge\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}_{i}\ ,

and we gauge it along the
\mathbb{T}^{3}
leaves of the foliation, i.e. we
introduce covariant derivatives on
\Sigma
only for the coordinates
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})

adapted to the leaves. The
procedure is formally identical to that described in
Section
5.2
: the self-duality constraint
\delta{\mathcal{S}}[\phi,A]/\delta A_{i}=0
leads to the reduced sigma-model

S[\phi]=\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\Theta}^{i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\Theta}^{j}

for the nilmanifold
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}},
with the image of
\phi
projected to the leaf space.
In other words, the Riemannian submersion described by the gauging is given by the bundle projection

\bar{\pi}:(M_{\mathsf{H}},{\mathcal{H}},\omega)\longrightarrow(M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}^{3},g,0)

where
M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}^{3}\simeq\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
, with background metric

(7.9)

g=\delta_{ij}\,\Theta^{i}\otimes\Theta^{j}=\mathrm{d}x\otimes\mathrm{d}x+(\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z)\otimes(\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z)+\mathrm{d}z\otimes\mathrm{d}z\ ,

and vanishing Kalb-Ramond field
b=0
; this reproduces the description
of
[
24
]
which was obtained using a different procedure (see
also
[
39
]
). We have thereby obtained the natural background on
the Heisenberg nilmanifold from the gauging of a generalized isometry
induced by the foliation given by the fibers of the principal

\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle
M_{{\mathsf{H}}}.
In our framework, we deal with globally defined sections of tensor bundles over
M_{{\mathsf{H}}}
and the reduced metric
g
on the quotient
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
is still a globally defined section.

7.3.
Strongly T-Dual Sigma-Model with
H
-Flux

We shall now show how the expected geometric T-dual background with NS–NS

H
-flux emerges within our framework. Again we will closely follow

[
24
,
39
]
. For this, we pull back the Born structure
(K,\eta,{\mathcal{H}})
, characterizing the nilmanifold polarization, by an

{\mathsf{O}}(3,3)(M_{\mathsf{H}})
-transformation
\vartheta
to get a polarization
which is defined as follows. We consider again a free
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-action on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
, which now however gives a principal
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle

\bar{\pi}^{\prime}{}:M_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{3}\ .

The short exact sequence of vector bundles induced by this principal bundle is

(7.10)

0\longrightarrow L_{\tt v}^{\prime}(M_{\mathsf{H}})\longrightarrow TM_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow\bar{\pi}^{\prime}{}^{*}(T\mathbb{T}^{3})\longrightarrow 0

and we choose an isotropic splitting

\bar{s}^{\prime}:\bar{\pi}^{\prime}{}^{*}(T\mathbb{T}^{3})\longrightarrow TM_{\mathsf{H}}

with respect to
\eta.
This defines an almost para-Hermitian structure
(K^{\prime},\eta)
on
M_{\mathsf{H}}.
The compatible generalized metric
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}{}
is defined by the horizontal lift of the standard Euclidean metric on the torus
\mathbb{T}^{3}.

Locally we may describe this polarization by the Lie algebra

(7.11)

[Z^{\prime}_{x},Z^{\prime}_{z}]=m\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,y}\ ,\quad[Z^{\prime}_{x},Z^{\prime}_{y}]=-m\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,z}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad[Z^{\prime}_{z},Z^{\prime}_{y}]=m\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,x}

of generators for the left-invariant vector fields on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
, with all other brackets vanishing; here we rearranged the
generators of the Drinfel’d double group
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
, so that
\{Z^{\prime}_{m},\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,m}\}
defines a new frame for the sections of
TM_{{\mathsf{H}}}
. This defines a new Born structure
(K^{\prime},\eta,{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}).
It may be regarded as a different choice of horizontal sub-bundle of
TM_{{\mathsf{H}}},
since the vertical distribution spanned by
\{\tilde{Z}^{m}\}
remains unchanged so the fibers are still 3-tori
\mathbb{T}^{3}.

The new eigenbundles are thus spanned by globally defined vector fields

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\ ,\quad Z^{\prime}_{y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial y}-m\,x\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad Z^{\prime}_{z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial z}-m\,y\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}}+m\,x\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}}\ ,\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}}\ .

The dual 1-forms are given explicitly by

\displaystyle\Theta^{\prime\,x}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}x\ ,\quad\Theta^{\prime\,y}=\mathrm{d}y\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\Theta^{\prime\,z}=\mathrm{d}z\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{x}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}+m\,y\,\mathrm{d}z\ ,\quad\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{y}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}+m\,z\,\mathrm{d}x\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{z}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}+m\,x\,\mathrm{d}y\ .

Analogously to the discussion in
[
24
]
, the action of the Lie algebroid
represented by
\{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\,i}\}
on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
generates an action of

\mathbb{T}^{3}
on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
; this highlights the existence of the structure of

M_{\mathsf{H}}
as a principal
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle given by

\bar{\pi}^{\prime}:M_{\mathsf{H}}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{3},
with
M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}^{3}\simeq\mathbb{T}^{3}.
Such a polarization is given by an isotropic splitting of the
canonical short exact sequence of vector bundles associated with any
fiber bundle, so that

TM_{\mathsf{H}}=L^{\prime}_{\tt h}(M_{\mathsf{H}})\oplus L^{\prime}_{\tt v}(M_{\mathsf{H}})\ .

There is a corresponding Born sigma-model
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}({\mathcal{H}}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
defined
by the new Born structure on
M_{\mathsf{H}}
obtained from the action of

\vartheta\in{{\mathsf{O}}}(3,3)(M_{\mathsf{H}})
on the nilmanifold polarization, with

{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}=\delta_{ij}\,\Theta^{\prime\,i}\otimes\Theta^{\prime\,j}+\delta^{ij}\,\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{j}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\omega^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime\,i}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\ .

The metric
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}
is a bundle-like metric obtained from the horizontal
lift
g^{\prime}_{+}=\delta_{ij}\,\Theta^{\prime\,i}\otimes\Theta^{\prime\,j}

of the standard Euclidean metric on
\mathbb{T}^{3}.
The fundamental 2-form

\omega^{\prime}
has non-trivial monodromies under the identification
x\sim x+1
,
y\sim y+1
and
z\sim z+1
, under which it changes by
B_{+}
-transformations in the
group of large diffeomorphisms of the doubled twisted torus
M_{\mathsf{H}}
. Comparing the corresponding field strength

{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=\mathrm{d}\omega^{\prime}=3\,m\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}z

to (
6.4
) shows that the only non-vanishing flux in this new
polarization is an
H
-flux
H^{\prime}_{xyz}=m
.

The Born sigma-model then reads

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}[\phi]=\frac{1}{4}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\Big(\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\Theta}^{\prime\,i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\Theta}^{\prime\,j}+\delta^{ij}\,\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{j}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\bar{\Theta}^{\prime\,i}\wedge\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{i}\ .

In this case we consider again the sub-bundle
L_{\tt v}^{\prime}(M_{\mathsf{H}})
as Lie
algebroid for the gauging. It is easy to show that

\pounds_{{X}_{\tt v}^{\prime}}\,g^{\prime}_{+}=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\pounds_{{X}_{\tt v}^{\prime}}\,\omega^{\prime}(Y_{\tt h},Z_{\tt h})=0\ ,

for all
{X}_{\tt v}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{\tt v}^{\prime}(M_{\mathsf{H}}))
and
Y_{\tt h},Z_{\tt h}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L^{\prime}_{\tt h}(M_{\mathsf{H}})).

Thus the gauging can be implemented by covariantizing the pullback
differentials of the leaf coordinates
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
in the usual way and the reduced sigma-model on
the leaf space
M_{{\mathsf{H}}}/\mathbb{T}^{3}\simeq\mathbb{T}^{3}
is given by
S^{\prime}(g^{\prime},b^{\prime})

with the background

g^{\prime}=\mathrm{d}x\otimes\mathrm{d}x+\mathrm{d}y\otimes\mathrm{d}y+\mathrm{d}z\otimes\mathrm{d}z\ ,

and

b^{\prime}=-m\,(x\,\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}z+y\,\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}x+z\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y)\ .

The Kalb-Ramond field
b^{\prime}
is only locally defined on
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, but the
topological term of the sigma-model can be defined by a Wess-Zumino
extension using
the corresponding
H
-flux

H^{\prime}=\mathrm{d}b^{\prime}=-3\,m\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}z\ ,

which is a globally defined integral 3-form on
\mathbb{T}^{3}.

In other words, we obtain a reduced sigma-model on the leaf space
described by the Riemannian submersion

\bar{\pi}^{\prime}:(M_{{\mathsf{H}}},{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\longrightarrow(M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}^{3},g^{\prime},b^{\prime})\ ,

with
M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}^{3}\simeq\mathbb{T}^{3}
,
which is again the projection map of the principal
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle
associated with this polarization. This is an explicit example of how
standard T-dual geometric backgrounds, in this case
(\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}},g,b=0)
and
(\mathbb{T}^{3},g^{\prime},b^{\prime})
, emerge from our formalism in the spirit
of Section
4.4
.

7.4.
T-Fold Polarization

We shall now describe the standard T-fold background which is T-dual
to the
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
and
\mathbb{T}^{3}
backgrounds within our formalism. So far
we considered the two natural
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-actions on the doubled twisted
torus
M_{\mathsf{H}}.
Let us now discuss what happens when we try to use the
leaf space of the
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
foliation for the Lie algebroid action. Here

M_{\mathsf{H}}
is again foliated by both
\mathbb{T}^{3}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}},
but the
distributions identified with their tangent bundles now have opposite
eigenvalues to those of the nilmanifold polarization from
Section
7.2
. In this case we cannot work with any
fibration, since
M_{\mathsf{H}}
does not admit the structure of a principal

\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
-bundle, similarly to the discussion of
[
24
]
.
We shall describe how to define the new para-Hermitian structure
starting from the natural left-invariant Riemannian metric
{\mathcal{H}}
in
this polarization.

For instance, let us consider the global coframe

\displaystyle\Theta^{x}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}x\ ,\quad\Theta^{y}=\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\Theta^{z}=\mathrm{d}z+m\,x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{x}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}-m\,\tilde{z}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ ,\quad\tilde{\Theta}_{y}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{\Theta}_{z}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\ ,

descending from the left-invariant 1-forms on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}},
where

(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
are local coordinates adapted to
the
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
foliation. Then the Riemannian metric
{\mathcal{H}}
descending
from the left-invariant Riemannian metric on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
is given in
this local basis by

{\mathcal{H}}=\delta_{ij}\,\Theta^{i}\otimes\Theta^{j}+\delta^{ij}\,\tilde{\Theta}_{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{j}\ .

To define the new almost para-Hermitian structure associated with the
T-fold polarization, we consider the natural foliations of the doubled
twisted torus, just as we did in Section
7.2
. In that
case, we saw that there is a natural almost para-Hermitian structure
arising from the principal
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle structure of
M_{\mathsf{H}},
since
there we were interested in the quotient with respect to the
\mathbb{T}^{3}
foliation. We now wish to discuss the outcome of the other possible quotient, given by the
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
foliation.

Since there is no other natural fiber bundle structure in this case,
we use the left-invariant metric
{\mathcal{H}}
to define the splitting we
need. For this, we consider the short exact sequence of vector bundles
associated with the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}
by
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
:

0\longrightarrow T{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow TM_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow N\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow 0\ .

We then choose an isotropic splitting

s:N\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}\longrightarrow TM_{\mathsf{H}}

whose image
{\rm Im}(s)=T{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}^{\perp}
is the orthogonal
complement of the tangent bundle
T{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}
with respect to
{\mathcal{H}}
; it
is maximally isotropic with respect to the split signature metric
\eta

inherited from the Drinfel’d double structure on
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
.

Let
\{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,i}\}=\{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,x},\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,y},\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,z}\}
be a basis of left-invariant vector
fields on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
with dual 1-forms
\{\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\}=\{\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime\prime}_{x},\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime\prime}_{y},\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime\prime}_{z}\}.
Then
{\rm Im}(s)
is locally generated
by the vector fields

Z^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}+\frac{1}{2}\,N^{\prime\prime}_{ij}\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,j}\ ,

where

N^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}=\frac{2\,m\,x}{1+(m\,x)^{2}}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&-1\\
0&1&0\end{pmatrix}\ .

This basis is completed by the vector fields
\{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,j}\}
to form a local frame for
\mathsf{\Gamma}(TM_{\mathsf{H}}).
Then the dual coframe is given by

\Theta^{\prime\prime\,i}=\mathrm{d}x^{i}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\tilde{\Theta}_{i}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,N^{\prime\prime}_{ji}\,\mathrm{d}x^{j}

and the Riemannian metric
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime\prime}
in this coframe has the form

{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}g^{\prime\prime}_{+}&0\\
0&g^{\prime\prime}_{-}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

where the local expressions for
g^{\prime\prime}_{+}
and
g^{\prime\prime}_{-}
are

g^{\prime\prime}_{+}=\frac{1}{1+(m\,x)^{2}}\,\begin{pmatrix}1+(m\,x)^{2}&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad g^{\prime\prime}_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1+(m\,x)^{2}&0\\
0&0&1+(m\,x)^{2}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

with
g^{\prime\prime}_{+}
written in the coframe
\{\Theta^{\prime\prime\,i}\}=\{\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z\}
,
and
g^{\prime\prime}_{-}
written in the coframe
\{\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\}

which is globally defined on the Heisenberg nilmanifold

\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
. The fiberwise Riemannian metric
g^{\prime\prime}_{+}
on
T{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}^{\perp}={\rm Im}(s)
is transverse invariant,
i.e.
\pounds_{{X}_{-}}\,g^{\prime\prime}_{+}=0,
for all
{X}_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}})
, and
{\rm Ker}(g^{\prime\prime}_{+})={\rm Im}(s)
by definition of the splitting
s.
Therefore
(M_{\mathsf{H}},g^{\prime\prime}_{+},\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}})
is a Riemannian foliation.

In this local frame, we can use the functions
N^{\prime\prime}_{ij}
to also write down the local
decomposition of the fundamental 2-form

\omega^{\prime\prime}
, as
discussed in Section
4.3
. Comparing its field strength

{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime\prime}=\mathrm{d}\omega^{\prime\prime}
with (
6.4
) identifies both non-vanishing

Q
-flux and
H
-flux in this polarization:

(7.12)

Q^{\prime\prime\,yz}{}_{x}=-m\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad H^{\prime\prime}_{xyz}=\frac{m\,\big(1-(m\,x)^{2}\big)}{\big(1+(m\,x)^{2}\big)^{2}}\ .

The local forms of both the compatible generalized metric
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime\prime}
and
the fundamental 2-form

\omega^{\prime\prime}
should be understood in a continuation of
x\in[0,1)
to a
covering space
\mathbb{R}_{x}
of the
x
-circle
\mathbb{S}_{x}^{1}
. Under the
identification
x\sim x+1
describing the covering map
\mathbb{R}_{x}\to\mathbb{S}_{x}^{1}
, they change by an

{\mathsf{O}}(3,3)(M_{\mathsf{H}})
-transformation in the group of large
diffeomorphisms of the doubled twisted torus
M_{\mathsf{H}}
.

The Lie algebroid to be used for the gauging is
T{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}.
It is
easy to show that the Born sigma-model
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime\prime}({\mathcal{H}}^{\prime\prime},\omega^{\prime\prime})

satisfies the generalized isometry conditions with respect to
\{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime\,m}\},
i.e. the Lie derivative of

\omega^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}
also has vanishing orthogonal component, with
respect to the
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathsf{H}}
, along any vector field on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
.
This yields the Riemannian submersion

{\mathit{\Pi}}^{\prime\prime}:(M_{{\mathsf{H}}},{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime\prime},\omega^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow(M_{{\mathsf{H}}}/\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}},g^{\prime\prime},b^{\prime\prime})

with

g^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{1+(m\,x)^{2}}\,\begin{pmatrix}1+(m\,x)^{2}&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad b^{\prime\prime}=\frac{m\,x}{1+(m\,x)^{2}}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&-1\\
0&1&0\end{pmatrix}

in the holonomic basis for 1-forms
\{\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z\}.
In this
case we recover the standard non-geometric background
(g^{\prime\prime},b^{\prime\prime})

T-dual to the previous
\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
and
\mathbb{T}^{3}
backgrounds, with

H
-flux
H^{\prime\prime}=\mathrm{d}b^{\prime\prime}
as in (
7.12
) (up to a sign). Here the quotient space

M_{{\mathsf{H}}}/\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
is an example of a leaf space which does
not have the structure of a smooth manifold. In particular, since

(M_{\mathsf{H}},g^{\prime\prime}_{+},\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}})
is a Riemannian foliation with compact
leaves, the leaf space
M_{\mathsf{H}}/\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
is an orbifold

[
71
,
81
,
93
]
. Thus the quotient map
{\mathit{\Pi}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}:M_{\mathsf{H}}\rightarrow M_{{\mathsf{H}}}/\mathbb{T}_{\mathsf{H}}
is an orbifold submersion.

7.5.
Essentially Doubled Polarization

Similarly to the T-fold polarization, in the essentially doubled
polarization we consider the principal
\mathbb{T}^{3}
-bundle
\bar{\pi}^{\prime}{}:M_{\mathsf{H}}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{3}
and try to describe the (now disallowed) gauging with respect to the distribution
{\rm Im}(\bar{s}^{\prime})
given by the isotropic splitting of the short exact sequence of vector bundles (
7.10
) induced by the principal fibration with respect to the split signature metric
\eta.
In other words, in this polarization there is only one integrable distribution on
M_{{\mathsf{H}}}
whose induced foliation has leaves
\mathbb{T}^{3},
while
{\rm Im}(\bar{s}^{\prime})
is a non-involutive sub-bundle of
TM_{\mathsf{H}}
. Then the allowed gauging along the foliation would simply give the naive T-dual of the polarization with NS–NS
H
-flux from Section
7.3
, obtained locally by interchanging the coordinates
(x,y,z)
and
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
; indeed, it is only from this perspective that the essentially doubled background satisfies the strong constraint of double field theory, as discussed by
[
41
]
.

We may follow the same steps of Section
5.5
and discuss why
it is not possible to recover a conventional reduced sigma-model even
locally. Again this relies on the fact that the vector sub-bundle

L_{-}={\rm Im}(\bar{s}^{\prime})
is non-integrable in this polarization, so the
only foliation present is given by
L_{+}=L^{\prime}_{\tt v}(M_{\mathsf{H}}),
which gives the
naive T-dual of the sigma-model for the spacetime
\mathbb{T}^{3}
with
H
-flux.
This polarization is characterized by globally defined coframes

\displaystyle\Theta^{R\,x}=\mathrm{d}x-m\,\tilde{z}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ ,\quad\Theta^{R\,y}=\mathrm{d}y-m\,\tilde{x}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\Theta^{R\,z}=\mathrm{d}z+m\,\tilde{x}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}

for
L_{+}^{*},
and

\tilde{\Theta}^{R}_{x}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\ ,\quad\tilde{\Theta}^{R}_{y}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tilde{\Theta}^{R}_{z}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}

for
L_{-}^{*}.

The compatible generalized metric is given
as usual by

{\mathcal{H}}^{R}=\delta_{ij}\,\Theta^{R\,i}\otimes\Theta^{R\,j}+\delta^{ij}\,\tilde{\Theta}^{R}_{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}^{R}_{j}

and the fundamental 2-form is

\omega^{R}=\Theta^{R\,i}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}^{R}_{i}\ ,

with field strength

{\mathcal{K}}^{R}=\mathrm{d}\omega^{R}=3\,m\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\ .

Comparing with (
6.4
) thus shows that this polarization is
characterized by a single non-vanishing
R
-flux

(7.13)

R^{xyz}=m\ .

The corresponding Born sigma-model then reads

{\mathcal{S}}^{R}[\phi]=\frac{1}{4}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\Big(\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\Theta}^{R\,i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\Theta}^{R\,j}+\delta^{ij}\,\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}^{R}_{i}\wedge\star\,\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}^{R}_{j}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\bar{\Theta}^{R\,i}\wedge\bar{\tilde{\Theta}}^{R}_{i}\ .

As discussed in Section
5.5
, there is no foliation in this
case whose adapted local coordinates are
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z}),
and therefore we can only force the gauging of these
naive dual coordinates (with no geometric interpretation) by introducing “covariantized” maps

\mathrm{D}^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{\tilde{x}}_{i}=\mathrm{d}\bar{\tilde{x}}_{i}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\ ,

where
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
are the components of the tensor induced by the vector
bundle morphism
\bar{{\mathcal{A}}}:T\Sigma\rightarrow\phi^{*}L_{-}
covering
the identity. The “gauged” Born sigma-model is written in the usual way
and eliminating the auxiliary fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
through their equations
of motion
\delta{\mathcal{S}}^{R}[\phi,{\mathcal{A}}]/\delta{\mathcal{A}}_{i}=0
gives the
self-duality constraints

\delta_{ij}\,R^{ikl}\,\bar{\tilde{x}}_{k}\,\star\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{j}+\delta_{ij}\,R^{ikl}\,\bar{\tilde{x}}_{k}\,R^{jmn}\,\bar{\tilde{x}}_{m}\,\star\,\mathrm{D}^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{\tilde{x}}_{n}+\mathrm{d}\bar{x}^{l}+R^{ikl}\,\bar{\tilde{x}}_{k}\,\mathrm{D}^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{\tilde{x}}_{i}=0\ ,

where the components of the antisymmetric
R
-flux structure constants
are given by (
7.13
) and in this equation there is no sum
over the indices
k,m.
Similarly to
[
24
]
, solving this constraint for

\mathrm{D}^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{\tilde{x}}_{i}
eliminates all dependence on
\mathrm{d}\bar{\tilde{x}}_{i}
, but this does not give a
local reduced sigma-model which is independent of the naive dual
coordinates
\tilde{x}_{i}
. Thus the only possible gauging leads to the
naive T-dual of the sigma-model for the 3-torus
\mathbb{T}^{3}
with
H
-flux, i.e. the reduced sigma-model
S^{R}(g^{R},b^{R})
obtained from writing the reduced
sigma-model
S^{\prime}(g^{\prime},b^{\prime})
of Section
7.3
locally in the
coordinates
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
instead of
(x,y,z)
,
so that the background is now given by

g^{R}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\otimes\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}+\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\otimes\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}+\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\otimes\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\ ,

and

b^{R}=-m\,(\tilde{x}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}+\tilde{y}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}+\tilde{z}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{y})\ .
